First of all, I would like to say that among the objectives of the National School of Judiciary, the ECHR, is the continuing training of judicial officers. In this context, the ECHR organises training seminars, conferences, conferences, conferences, conferences, which take part as rapporteurs in active judicial proceedings, members of senior education institutions, specialist scientists, and even lawyers with at least 15 years of legal service. The selection of the rapporteurs is by decision of the relevant curriculum, at the request of the training and training director in the same direction, on the basis of specific criteria, as laid down in the law on the RIS. You realise that the political leadership of the Ministry of Justice, respecting the personal and operational independence of judicial officers, as well as of course the academic freedom of the ECHR, cannot control - it is self-evident - the content of the recommendations announced. It goes without saying, too, that each rapporteur can freely express his scientific opinion on questions which he raises in his report. Accordingly, any of the participants in the training seminar may formulate their own, any different view of the recommendation, in order that through the dialectic shape of a scientific contrast between the speech and the counterpart, scientific dialogue may be promoted. So - to make it quite clear - the current government - I want to be honest with you - neither draws up guidelines for the handling of scientific reports or judicial cases, nor intervenes and intervenes in the scientific opinion expressed by each rapporteur. As far as this is concerned, on the basis of the information that I have personally from the School's address, in fact at the headquarters of the ECHR in Thessaloniki on 5, 6 and 7 February 2020, the training seminar entitled “The new peacekeepers” took place. Our judges are unbiased, unaffected, and with an absolute sense of law, they are in one particular dispute at a time on the basis of the facts presented in the proceedings and which fall within the applicable rule of law. Mr President, I will return to my speech and tell you exactly what the thoughts are and what the next steps are. Because, I think, that's why we need to talk about it in the end. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-07-29-S1-commons.u54	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, there is no doubt at all that the period that we have lived in recent years has been unprecedented for all of us. Faced with the health and social crisis caused by the coronary pandemic was a test for society, a challenge for the state and the government. The daily lives of the citizens have changed. The relationship between the state and the citizen has been tested and forged. It has been difficult to make decisions, but they have brought results. The government's priority was to protect human life. It has managed in time and boldly and decisively to this very serious situation. Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis understood the problem in time and did not hesitate to order brave and difficult measures. The determination and daring of the Prime Minister and the government have brought our country to be among the best performing countries in terms of the management of the pandemic. At the same time, the reforms launched by the government before the crisis and targeted pandemic interventions have kept the recession on the European Union average at very low levels since the first quarter of 2020. Thus, within this short period of time of the crisis, measures were taken first to strengthen the public health system, support employment and the liquidity of businesses. Sadly, however, the crisis is still here and maintains at the forefront policies on the sustainability of enterprises, employment support, the safeguarding of liquidity in the market, in order to address the shocks caused by the economy and to keep unemployment low. Settlements that are not easy to calculate yet. We realise that the consequences will be great and will relate to the whole society, that we have to face enormous difficulties, that the strengths of society, the finances of the state, the labour and social relations are being hardened. However, our Government is moving on with plan and confidence, it has a strategy and is determined to deplete every financial margin so that it supports businesses, workers, pensioners, every citizen affected by the pandemic crisis. The financial staff and the government, moving in this direction, have tabled the bill we are debating today in the House and are coming to face the economic consequences of the crisis, to support the Greek economy, businesses and farmers and to relieve the households who have been so hurt and tested in the last few years since the pandemic crisis. In this context, the provisions aimed at attracting investment and creating a favourable tax climate are introduced. These are provisions that strengthen the affected social groups, with targeted tax exemptions, with the aim of supporting jobs and strengthening the competitiveness of the Greek economy. Interventions which always take account of them and of the environmental objectives, but also the policies of the government. Natural persons are encouraged to transfer their tax residence from a foreign country to Greece at a tax rate of 7% for 15 years and with exemption from the special solidarity levy, with the conditions that they are not tax residents of Greece, five out of the last six years before transferring their tax residence to Greece and transferring their tax residence from a country with which our country has an administrative cooperation agreement in the field of tax cooperation. The introduction of this measure, which is already being implemented successfully in several European Union countries, will help increase public revenue. The bill provides for exemption from the free disposal of shares and operative domestic tourism from employee income tax. This regulation is particularly favourable to both workers and businesses, particularly to tourism enterprises which have been particularly affected by the pandemic crisis. In this connection, the possibility of deducting any costs incurred by the sacred monasteries of Mount Athos from income from renting immovable property. Thus, the religious and charitable work of the sacred monks is strengthened. The exemption in force in respect of the property tax and the taxation of donations relating to movable assets of Greek residents resident abroad is extended. Incentives to purchase first homes shall be strengthened. This is done by abolishing the self-taxation of money donations and parental benefits from parents to children to buy first home. This recognises the true contribution of parents to supporting and addressing family needs. At the same time, however, it is also a support and a boost to building activity. The suspension of a trade fee for farmers of normal VAT arrangements and coastal fishermen is laid down. The government recognises the primary sector's contribution to our economy and supports the efforts of these people, farmers and fishermen to cope with the difficult situation they have experienced as a result of the pandemic crisis. In addition, a new system of progressive passenger car taxation is introduced. With the reintroduction of the progressive taxation in relation to the escalator, which was the case with the previous law, the way in which vehicles are taxed is fairer. What does that practically mean? With the law that the SYRIZA government passed in 2016, if a used car had a customs value of 14,000 euros, it had a 4% registration fee. If the same car of the same old age, with an extra element of equipment and a tariff-rate difference of EUR 1, 2, 3 or 5, would pay a registration fee of 8% over the whole amount, i.e. twice the registration fee for the same car. With the draft legislation, the citizen will pay twice the registration fee only for the excess amount, namely EUR 1, 2,3,5 in excess of EUR 14,000. In other words, cars of the same old age and category will be treated exactly the same way. This means that the law becomes fairer and constitutional, because it also meets the principle of proportionality. So we have a reduction in the corresponding registration fee for the majority of vehicles, an increase in the State's revenues and, above all, a reduction in cars from 1,400 cubic metres to 2,000 cubic metres - cars which are also the cars used by the average citizen - and of course a benefit to the citizen. It is also abrogated by this draft law and the provision for the application of the historical fee, a provision which has brought too many problems to a close of the process and protests because there were delays of six, eight and ten months, but also a loss of revenue for the State. The discount of 75% for caravans creates a new car market and allows for car tourism to develop in our country as well. In simple terms, for a self-propelled caravan that the citizen has had to pay EUR 10,000 so far, with the provision introduced in this draft law will pay EUR 2 500. The same, Minister - we have also told the committees - could also apply to the ambulances. It could also - and it is a proposal - abolish the environmental ending of EUR 1 000 for Euro 5 cars only for agricultural and business vehicles. Incentives are being made to renew the fleet of cars that are circulating with new environmentally friendly cars. More generally, the introduction of new cars is favoured and the market in cars is stimulated. At the same time, the registration fee for low-polluter hybrid cars is further reduced, as well as for full exemption for electric cars. In this way, the competitiveness of the shipping-related business is enhanced. Exemptions shall be granted from customs duties, excise duties and excise duties on goods used for the supply of professional ships. The VAT rate on sports games tickets is reduced. The aim is to maintain, at a low level, or even to reduce, the final prices of tickets, as well as to stimulate these demand. Therefore, it is indirectly supported in this way by the teams, the AOCs, the sports clubs, which have also been affected by the pandemic crisis. Income tax advance for tax year 2019 shall be reduced. Important intervention to enhance the viability of enterprises through increased business liquidity. Interventions in the field of out-of-court settlement of pending tax disputes before the courts are important. They aim to resolve disputes quickly, to dismantle administrative justice and stimulate tax revenue. European directives are incorporating, concerning the establishment of a tax dispute resolution mechanism at European Union level. Administrative cooperation between the Member States of the European Union is envisaged against aggressive tax planning and the introduction of rules against tax evasion. On the basis of these new rules, the aim is to better determine the taxable income in Greece, but also in the other countries - members of the European Union. The provisions of the draft law shall be amended and the framework for the functioning of the Law Council of the State shall be defined. Finally, one of the most important chapters of this draft law may be the legislation concerning the public contribution to repayment of loans for first home borrowers affected by the adverse effects of the krone. These arrangements are summarised in the ‘GEYRA’ programme on the protection of first residence. This is a programme which is considerably enlarged in relation to Law 4605/2019 of the previous government. The SYRIZA Government, in February 2019, abolished the protection of first residence. It then formed a framework which did not guarantee the protection of all borrowers. So we inherited a framework involving loans, which were not served until 31-12-2018. The Government of New Democracy is setting up a new loan grant programme including up-to-date borrowers. This is a programme whereby the State will grant a significant proportion of the monthly instalments for housing and business loans, with a mortgage on the principal residence of the people affected by the consequences of the coronary. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I shall conclude, Mr President. More specifically, the GEOFYRA programme is taking an important step towards the management of private debt. It broadens the type of beneficiaries and covers all categories of people affected by the consequences of the pandemic. The new arrangement includes natural persons, workers in sectors affected and freelance professionals, unemployed who receive exceptional support, natural persons with a monthly wage reduction, professionals who have had a reduction in their business activity - that is, they have had a reduction of more than 20% of VAT - in the second quarter of 2020 compared to the corresponding quarter of 2019, beneficiaries who received aid in the form of a repayable advance, natural persons who are part of the ‘COMPAN - WORK’ employment support mechanism, property owners receiving a reduced rental. For all the above categories of persons affected, State aid is granted for both services and non-performing loans which reach up to EUR 600 on a case-by-case basis. The programme's innovation is that for the first time, consistent borrowers are rewarded with serviced loans, which have been affected by the pandemic. This supports all borrowers, strengthens social cohesion, supports vulnerable social classes, cultivates payment culture and reduces the risk of creating a new generation of red loans because of the pandemic. To sum up, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that the bill we are debating today before the House is yet another successful intervention by the Government and the Ministry of Finance. The financial staff, interpreting the consequences of the crisis, the needs and problems of society, drafts a bill that comes to support those affected by the economic crisis. It gives solutions to issues relating to the Greek people, strengthens the sense of security, social cohesion and justice. Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis and the Government of New Democracy, consistent with their commitments, with effective interventions support the Greek family, entrepreneurship, work and even very difficult times, such as the ones we live today, concerning reforms aimed at improving public administration for the benefit of the entire Greek people. This objective serves this draft law, which, of course, we support and call on all the groups in this House to do the same. Thank you.

In order to categorise the objective nature of the offence referred to in Article 184 PK, the law requires the perpetrator to provoke or stimulate the committing of a crime or crime and thereby jeopardise public order. A burdensome occasion shall be considered, when the challenge of stirring a defect or a felony is directed against a group identified on the basis of race characteristics, colour, ethnic or ethnic origin, genealogical outcomes, religion, disability, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender characteristics. In addition, the law is seen as targeting broader social groups on the basis of strictly limited criteria, including race, colour, ethnic or ethnic origin, genealogical outcomes, religion, disability, sexual orientation, gender characteristics, but not the profession. The objective of the offence in question is therefore not fulfilled. Furthermore, in order to quantify the objective nature of the offences referred to in Articles 1, 3 of the anti-racist law, the law requires the alleged perpetrator, public, orally, or through the press, via the Internet or in any other way to motivate, provoke, stimulate or encourage acts or actions that can discriminatory hatred or violence against a person or group of persons, identified by race, colour, religion, etc., but not the profession. Likewise, in the case of the offence referred to in Article 185 PK, my comment is not objectively appropriate to motivate, provoke, stimulate or promote acts of violence against anyone. Furthermore, here the law strictly defines and defines the criteria for targeting the wider social groups against which the act and occupation are not within them. The objective of the offence, for which I am being indicted and in the case-file, is not fulfilled, therefore, is an extension of the provision, which is unacceptable to criminal law. Subsequently, for the offence of slandering, the law on the haunting of the objective substance requires at least the dissemination of an event and, indeed, that this should be specific and objectively false and not, of course, the reproduction of a stage in the context above. Secondly, it requires a person at the expense of whom he specifically refers and not generally and abstractly. In my comment I did not share any facts that could harm the price or reputation of any particular person and, of course, this comment is not addressed to a certain person. On this critical constitutional and legal issue, I take the following. First of all, it is clear that the legal position of Members is determined by the privileges offered by the Constitution to them, which they enjoy, such as immunities. The provisions of Articles 61 and 62 of the Constitution, which are an extension of the principle of the freedom of a Member's term of office, shall establish the parliamentary immunity which refers, on the one hand, to the irresponsibleness of the Member in the performance of his parliamentary duties and, on the other, to the special protection of him during the parliamentary term only from the exercise of criminal proceedings for offences relating to his or her parliamentary or political activity. Divorcely, ladies and gentlemen, both irresponsible and irresponsible are institutional guarantees. That is to say, not in favour of the Member, as a person, such as the individual rights laid down in their respective bodies, but in order to ensure the proper functioning of the democratic culture through the unrestricted exercise of the duties and activities of the Member, as divisively, I repeat, they are referred to in Rule 62. The beginning of these immunities is in English, they are in both the French Enlightenment and the French Revolution and, of course, in our first constitutions, of course, they exist throughout the entire period. Furthermore, in accordance with Rule 62 of the Constitution and Rule 83 of the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament, by which the case-file has been forwarded to the House, Parliament is required to refuse to grant permission to prosecute a criminal prosecution against a Member, where the act in respect of which the waiver of immunity is sought is linked to its parliamentary duties or to political activity or finds that the prosecution conceals political feasibility, in accordance with Rule 83 of the Rules of Procedure of the House. The notion of political activity, ladies and gentlemen, is wider than that of the Member of Parliament. The legislator, in other words, realises that the primary role of the Member, as an expressor and a modulator of the public, is under the present conditions of policy not only within Parliament but also in other forums of political dialogue. Such forums of political dialogue are parties, trade unions and other political events, as well as the media. The forum for dialogue today, however, are social media, a phenomenon that is researched scientifically in the field of political science, communication policy, media sociology and, of course, at the National and Kapodistrian University of Athens, is being studied scientifically in the field of postgraduate program, "Policy and Internet". It is a common place now that all politicians, parties and leaders are internationally using social media in order to highlight their political messages, express an opinion on issues or comment on political topicality. This is because their political message will reach everyone directly, without the use of the wooden political speech and with the interference of any other third person.

We had said so in the constitutional review procedure, when all the others and SYRIZA - who are washing them now - agreed and voted together with New Democracy and the other parties to revise Article 62. We cannot legitimise this practice. We cannot give the green light of persecution against MPs for any position they support. In this respect, we do not agree with the lifting of immunity for Mrs Cassimat's case, which is of a more general interpretative significance. For the other two cases, we agree on the lifting of immunity.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-07-29-S1-commons.u105	Thank you, Mr President. First of all, let me begin by saying that your decision to impose the use of the mask and within Parliament is medically correct and symbolically imperative. As you know, we are in the management phase of what some people might call in Europe the second wave of pandemic - here it has not yet made its appearance, the cases remain in very low numbers - but I would like to say once again from the Step of Parliament that it is absolutely essential to keep the advice of doctors and to wear our mask where experts have indicated that the use of the mask is mandatory. It is a small, uncomfortable practice at first, but a practice that should be done, as we now have enough medical data, which tell us that universal use of a mask prevents the spread of the coronary. We also have an obligation here in Parliament to set a good example. Of course, there may also be one step in the right direction, namely that the use of the mask restricts the holidays to speakers, which I think may be welcome for the general order of the debate. Ladies and gentlemen, I now come to the bill under discussion, and I begin by greeting its order, which is not directly linked to its main content. It is, however, a debt of honor to our Armed Forces, towards a whole generation of Hellenism and a bloody, but heroic page of our recent history. Forty-six years since the invasion of Cyprus, the Greek state awards a lifelong pension to men who defended freedom in the front line. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] We owed it to national conscience and historical memory and we did so. Ladies and gentlemen, it is today my first presence in the House, following the historic decision by Brussels on the establishment of the European Recovery Fund, the reaction to the consequences of the coronary and its response to those who rushed early, as it appeared, to question its power to overcome disputes and ultimately serve common supranational demands. The outcome of this four-day summit had an undeniable winner. The united and solidarity of Europe! [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] Because, despite the noise, the intense negotiations, the objections which have been made, not even a minute, and no one has called into question the substance of the fund. In other words, the issue of a common debt, in which members contribute over time, depending on their capacity, is directly allocated, as required. And the Recovery Fund is not just Europe's response to a virus that cost more than 100 thousand of our fellow citizens and has, of course, plunged Europe into the deepest economic recession in its history. It also marks the fall of a political taboo, which was to strengthen European states from a capital formed at supranational level by mutual guarantees. In this respect, yes, the agreement of 21 July is a historic moment for the development and deepening of the European Union. And it is no accident, therefore, that these days and other parliaments - the Spanish for example, have a debate today on the very same subject - are discussing this huge step. A financing of EUR 750 billion in the Member States. Greece is one of the winning countries of the agreement, as the net inputs it will receive are the highest in the Eurozone as a percentage of GDP and one of the largest in total in the European Union. If we add the funds in the multiannual financial framework, this is translated into a total of 72 billion, 32 from the newly created fund and about 40 from the European Union budget, primarily directed at the Common Agricultural Policy and the Cohesion Fund. Of these first 32 billion, 19 eventually will represent direct subsidies and 13 more favourable loans. I want to remind the National Representation that we have been from the first, from the protagonists in this effort. I personally signed a letter with eight more Heads of State and Government, which we had sent to President Michelle last March, asking him precisely then to take this bold step. And if we turn the clock of time back a little - and I would like to address those who, I would like to believe, have criticised the scope of European intervention four months ago, the step taken by the European family would have fantasied to many as a science fiction scenario. But ten days ago we ended up with exhaustive consultations in Brussels's success. I believe that we, too, have put our own bit, not only in order to safeguard the interests of the country, but in order to fight for the need for Europe to take this bold step as a whole. The objectives we had set in the first place as a country have been met in full. Who were they? Firstly, the fund budget has a very important fire force. Secondly, the mixture of capital granted should be of a nature more subsidies and less loans. Thirdly and very importantly, Europe must move quickly towards decision-making that will speed up the disbursement of funds by the beginning of 2021. I would remind you that we can use the fund's tools to cover expenditure that has also been incurred in 2020 and to extend the duration of the fund to three years, to make commitments, and to another three, to make disbursements. And fourthly, and very importantly, the allocation of these funds is not accompanied by additional anti-developmental, budgetary constraints. There is obviously no supplementary memorandum on the disbursement of these funds. The conditions are the same for all twenty-seven Member States of the European Union. We are now faced with a truly great national challenge. And that is no other than a different concept in how we manage these funds. The challenge is not to absorb these funds to provide only temporary relief. The crucial bet is that these resources should be directed properly, in actions with a substantial, developmental, multiplier effect. The bet is by the right measure, the right reason, at the right time and in the right way to transform what we called the 'Next Generation EU' into 'Next Generation Hellas', to convert the crisis as a new starting point for the country's overall direction and economy into an environment that will inspire and exploit all creative forces in our country, first of all, by offering security, good jobs, hospitable everyday life and social cohesion. In this context, we are working - I believe for the first time since post-polluterism and then on this extent and at this depth - a coherent national strategic plan for the optimal production and leverage of all available funds. The targeting and allocation of resources will not be done this time as usual, i.e. by uncomplicated procedures, delays in planning and absorption, a variety of customer interests that clearly introduce for resources in low added value programmes or projects. The government plan will be based on the proposals and strategic directions of the Pissaridis Committee, which incorporates all modern global trends and has made an accurate diagnosis of both the time-consuming distortions of our economy and its significant comparative advantages. In the next two months there will be an intensive consultation, both at government level, but also at Parliament's level and at the level of discussion with the social partners, so that we can reach as wide a consensus as possible, so that we can have a coherent national proposal, which we will submit for approval to the European Commission in October. I would remind you that the timetable set by the European Commission is sufficiently suffocating. By 15 October, the countries must have submitted their national plans, which will then be approved by the European Commission, so that we can lead to the disbursement of the first resources in 2021, within the first half of 2021. It is therefore a moment, Mr President, of maximum national responsibility. The government majority and the government will do everything they can, in order to get through a rapid consultation phase, to listen to the parties' views, to formulate, as I have said, a national coherent plan, which I do not have the requirement, obviously, of finding all parties in opposition. In any case, however, a debate will also take place within Parliament before the national plan is tabled, so that the House and the opposition parties can express their views, their views or possibly their objections. Ladies and gentlemen, a year ago from the very same position I was talking about the first bill of our Government, which was fulfilling a basic commitment from us, then, an election commitment, the reduction of the ENPI as a measure of tax relief and a development perspective. Twelve months later, I am here again for another bill from the Ministry of Finance, the first bill from the Ministry of the second year of our term of office, but which has the same aim: to combine tax justice with the very necessary economic situation in the country. To date, a debate in both the committee and in the House at the level of rapporteurs has been detailed. I will make a very brief summary, stressing a few articles, some provisions of the bill that I consider to be extremely critical. The first package of interventions in the bill relates to tax incentives. A favourable scheme for foreign pensioners is being introduced in order to transfer their tax base to Greece. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] From now on, their income from abroad will be borne by all sources with a tax of only 7%, making Greece much more attractive and public revenues much larger. In other words, we are aligning ourselves with other European countries. Perhaps the best example is Portugal, which has used similar tools to attract European pensioners, who, in the extension of such a measure, have rushed to buy permanent residence in the European South and spend an important part of their time, not necessarily in their summers, but very often in their winter in the Mediterranean. We want to do something like that in Greece too. And I believe that this measure, following on from the intervention we have made for 'Non-Dom', which concerns far more wealthy people who will choose Greece as their tax office, will have a very important development mark. At the same time, I would like to point out that we abolish the autonomous taxation of 10% on parental benefits and donations, we reduce the I.C. classification fees, hybrids, electricals, so that the fleet is renewed and the shares that are paid free of charge by the employer are now taxed as an additional fee, thus encouraging participatory entrepreneurship. I would like to focus particularly on this provision, which is very important to the startups community, which is largely based on the logic of workers' participation in the company's share structure precisely with such tax incentives. It is an important provision which the Ministry of Finance introduces in response - as a government - to a permanent demand from the community of newly created entrepreneurship in our country, which, despite the great difficulties - has recently been aroused. Of course, Vouchers of domestic tourism are excluded from the income of a company. The second wave of arrangements strengthens the policies of shielding the incomes of households and businesses tested by the coronary, with a lever to reduce taxes, which stimulates liquidity. This is how the relief is implemented up to the nullification of the tax advance for 2019, as we were committed to doing as a government. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] This is a good measure, which also meets the universal support of counter-population, which is quite obvious in such a difficult year, where companies are faced with much lower turbulence and obviously with the prospect of having no profitability within 2020. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] The excess VAT rate is finally passed on to music books - an unfinished past one which is corrected - to the reduced 13% of racing tickets, while, as transport is affected, the types of equipment and supply are exempted from tariffs. The third set of measures is faced with a time-consuming wound, tax differences. The draft law incorporates at its core important European directives on disputes at Community level, including our country into a common European framework to tackle tax evasion, and a committee on extrajudicial settlement is also set up for disputes pending before administrative justice. It is something that stimulates legal certainty, dismantles the courts and increases public revenues. Finally - will the Minister speak much more in detail on this issue - innovation of the bill is the new loan support mechanism for borrowers, the 'GEOFYRA' project, which, with the decisive public participation, will grant for nine months housing and business loans guaranteeing first residence. Since then, thousands of cases began to be accumulated in the courts, which often exploited conscious malicious payers, while both extensions achieved by the new government eventually had reduced participation. Applications for membership in the favourable context established by the previous government, which we supported, have only reached five thousand! The protection of the first residence, as established by the first government, has therefore failed in practice. On the other hand, it is interesting to see that in the last 12 months direct arrangements between banks and borrowers have been very encouraged and more than two hundred thousand loans amounting to 13 billion - most of them housing - have been arranged directly between the bank and the borrower. The "GEOFYRA" programme aims at restoring a definitive framework for the protection of the main residence, even by significantly increasing the number of beneficiaries, because the borrowers of the old arrangements are now added to a new category: workers or professionals from affected branches and new problematic loans. The regulation also applies to all loans, both red and service-based. The Minister will also return to his place in more detail. Ladies and gentlemen, today's initiatives come at a very difficult time. They are, however, only a link to a large chain of interventions in the economy. For the first time in the government he managed and supported real liquidity from small and medium-sized enterprises, which the truth is that they had difficulty accessing bank lending. It is a successful programme that will continue and, if there is a need, and we will judge this with the Ministry, there is always the possibility of further expansion. It relieved them of the immediate need to pay an amount of more than 150 million, while the suspension of loan instalments gave rise to two hundred and eighty-four thousand businesses and households. All this was decided to have a substantial effect and not easy impressions. As I have said many times before, our programming is in full progress, always keeping reserves for every unforeseeable. If, in order to be clear about the context in which it is being set, we will proceed by ourselves to what comes out of the public eye that the Pissaridis report provides for the privatisation of the second pillar of the insurance system and the restructuring of the labour market, which, in my opinion, will also create a new vicious circle of austerity but also enormous social tension, because they will break through once again, in a society which has been hurt over the last ten years, social cohesion. We will, however, have the opportunity to discuss this in detail, Prime Minister. But the crucial thing, with which I wish to close my remark on this, is that we must not overlook that the change in the correlation between grants and loans, at the same time that we will have a recession that is close to 12% of GDP this year, with no chance of a rapid recovery, will again create unfavourable conditions in the debt-government relationship. Our debt at the end of the year will probably exceed 200% of GDP, with what this may mean for the risk of a revival of the debt crisis. So, to be clear, I should firstly - and I told you a moment ago - that you should immediately table your plan and the Pissaridis report, but also your own positions, what you are adopting and what you are not, in order to have a real dialogue, put the political parties in place. The second is that, in our view, Greece today needs a bold strategic plan, progressively to bring the economy up after the pandemic - we have called it a 'new social contract' - which will include development prospects focusing on green growth and the digital transition of the economy, the development of important sectors of the economy, such as the agri-food sector and processing, the treatment of inequalities and precariousness that are becoming increasingly widespread and, of course, a strong and effective social state. Let us not forget the lessons of the EHS pandemic. Greece needs a plan that will give hope and perspective to the next generations that it will not pass what the previous generations have passed. Unfortunately, I do not see any intention on your part to move in such a direction, but here we will be talking. But I think, ladies and gentlemen, that the question of the protection of first residence is extremely critical. In 2015, the Government of SYRIZA formed an integrated strategy for managing and addressing the private debt issue, non-performing loans. This strategy was multifaceted, set specific targets and milestones and sent a message of seriousness and determination both within and outside the country. Thus, during the period 2015-2019 there has been significant progress in reforming the relevant legislation, thus creating a substantial and expanded arsenal to address the problem. At the same time, and despite the fact that much of these reforms took place under close surveillance and budgetary discipline, the framework established included a multitude of favourable arrangements for workers and professionals, small businesses, for economically weak people. For example, debts to public and public sector bodies have been increased and red tape has been expanded, as has been said, by Katselis - Stathakis. The main residence protection law was created, Law 4605/2019. All natural persons, as well as entrepreneurial loans, had the first place of residence. Indeed, it did not pay as much as we would like this platform. However, this is due to the fact that since you took over, you have not taken care to solve any bureaucratic problem of what was present throughout the whole operation of this platform. [[Applause from the SYRIZA wing]] We have also created the extrajudicial mechanism to regulate debts, bankruptcy law has been considerably modernised, the code of conduct of banks has been reformed in contrast - look out! - with your own position on the subject. And I am referring to the amnesty you have tabled with amendment to the pandemic period for bank officials, which has, of course, resulted in a fiasco, as the provisions are already unconstitutional by the courts. [[Applause from the SYRIZA wing]] A special private debt management secretariat was also set up, a framework of public debt regulation, social security institutions, municipalities, with multi-annual instalments and important incentives and benefits. These were our own interventions. Have they solved the problem definitively? No, but they have significantly improved the situation, resulting, on the one hand, in the de-escalation of non-performing loans, on the other hand, in the facilitation of a significant proportion of overdue persons, households and professionals. The rapporteur from Mera25, I think, began by taking half of his time to the initiative that we are taking for the Legal Council of State. I have to repeat or add some elements, because it seems that everyone is not watching what we have voted on in Greece. According to Dr. 238/2003 the filling of the chairs of judges of the Legal Council of State is carried out by means of a competitive procedure. I shall submit the relevant text in the Minutes. The notice shall indicate, inter alia, the number of posts. For the final result of the tender, a scoreboard of successful tenderers shall be drawn up in a ranking order. Appointing those who have achieved the invitation to tender shall be in accordance with the order of inclusion in the table. Positions vacant or recommended within one year following publication of the successful table shall be covered by the table and in their order of registration. That's probably not what some people in the law noticed. It is therefore foreseen that the ranking of the Legal Board of the State is to be carried out by means of a successful tender for the number of posts provided for in the notice. Further needs under the successful list may be met only within one year of the publication of the list. Regular tendering obligation is not provided for in the current legislation. It is obvious that this process serves to serve the service of the Law Council of the State with the most competent staff force following a worthwhile and transparent competitive procedure. The long-standing - I stress - extension of the classification table is at risk - and I think we will all agree here - to circumvent the purpose and the existing legislation governing the functioning of the Legal Council of State, excluding from the competitive process those who are interested in participating in it on equal, transparent terms, unless that is what you want. In 2018, the Law Council of the State opened an invitation to tender for the filling of fifty-six empty organic posts. The successful scoreboard was published on 24-2-2019. The power of the table according to what we said and with the law, the presidential decree, would expire on 11-4-2020. In view of the fact that in March the country experienced unprecedented conditions due to the health crisis, without at that time being able to conceive with relative certainty the development of the facts, it was considered necessary to extend the list of successful tenderers for the tender by one year, as you have said, in order to cover the direct operating needs of the State's Legal Board. This extension was implemented by a Ministerial Decision regulating in general the matters of operation of the Law Council of the State, as had emerged from COVID-19. This decision also had the legislative basis of the act of 20 March 2020. I'll submit both documents to the Minutes. It was therefore a complete exceptional measure aimed at dealing with exceptional and unforeseen circumstances. Taking into account the development of the Treaties and the facts and the gradual lifting of the restrictive measures imposed in June, a communication was made with the President of the Legal Council of State in order to identify the immediate needs of the Legal Council of State. That is to say, on the one hand, to cover the positions in respect of which the relevant notice has been issued and on the other hand, any further use of the list for position coverage may not exceed one year from its publication. This information is well known to all those involved in the competition procedure. It is true, however, that the lack of periodicity in the awarding of tenders gives rise to insecurity and burdens the personal costs which are undoubtedly borne by all those who are toiling for their introduction into the College of the Legal Council of State. However, the same insecurity is experienced by those who expect to participate in a competition on the Legal Council of State and who have not participated to date or have failed. To restore this uncertainty, therefore, the Treasury intends to examine, in cooperation with the Head of the Law Council of the State, the introduction of a provision for periodic tenders at regular intervals, probably every two years. ParlaMint-GR_2020-07-29-S1-commons.u205	With glory and honor we enter the list of Members. Mr Ljupis from New Democracy has the floor and Mrs Papanatsiou follows from SYRIZA. Mr. Lippe, you have the floor for seven minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2020-07-29-S1-commons.u206	Thank you, Mr President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, many have expected that the unforeseen financial consequences of the pandemic would completely overthrow the planning of the government. However, governmental work in the field of the economy focuses mainly on two axes. Firstly, to limit the effects of the economic recession and, secondly, to continuing structural changes based on the planning of the government and the announcements of Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis. The first major innovation of the bill is favourable taxation for pensioners who will transfer their tax residence to Greece. We are following a practice of other European countries and a 7% tax rate is introduced for pension incomes from abroad and up to 15 years. This gives a strong incentive to pensioners abroad to opt for the Greek tax system, thereby strengthening public revenues. It is important that it exempts shares and internal tourism from taxation, worth up to 300 euros, which are given free of charge by businesses to workers. Incentives to attain objectives, known bonuses, are to the benefit of both workers and businesses. In particular, internal tourism layouts are very necessary, at least in this period, to stimulate the serious tourist movement. A great stimulus to the sale of real estate and building activity shall be provided by the provision of Article 7. Parental benefits and donations to children will no longer be taxed in order to purchase first home. It was a common Greek practice of financing the first house of new couples by their parents, and is thus welcomed by the Greek State. A stable request from the rural world is met by the trade exemption for farmers who joined the VAT system five years ago and of course also for fishermen in coastal fishing. This is a move of practical support in the primary sector to Greek farmers and fishermen, the necessity of which, Minister, we stressed, and a question in this regard within the framework of parliamentary control. Following on from the draft adopted last week on the electromobility and promotion of the use of new non-pollutant cars, the provision of Article 9 comes. It reduces the registration fee for hybrid cars, while completely exempting purely electrical cars. An excellent initiative is that of setting up the Committee on External Economic Relations to resolve tax disputes. Those who appeal to administrative justice against tax burdens imposed on them are forced to wait many years for the irrevocable trial of their cases. This time-consuming judicial procedure is detrimental to both taxpayers and the public. The new committee will examine very promptly requests from citizens who have already appealed to justice. Thus several cases will be able to be closed out of court, as a compromise solution is to be found, in particular, in cases of limitation of the right of the public or manifest error on the part of the tax authority. The draft law provides for a reduction or exemption from the advance payment of income tax for 2019. In particular, the favourable regulation concerns those who have recorded a reduction in VAT turnover between the first half of 2020 compared to that of 2019. This is a tangible demonstration of the support that companies receive in this difficult economic situation. Finally, the Ministry of Finance created the "GEYRA" programme for the effective protection of first residence. The government fully perceived the economic pressure on households and businesses, formed a protective grid for first residence. For nine months, the Greek State will participate in the payment of loans that have as collateral a mortgage on the principal residence of the debtor. This is a regulation that has been extended to almost every category of borrower. However, the great innovation of the new programme, which makes it completely innovative, is that it also concerns loans for service. For the first time, they will accept state support and borrowers, who have remained consistent with their credit obligations despite difficulties. In recent years, we have identified the tax bills with new taxes, a burden on households and businesses, and in any case with bad news for citizens. Today is not one of them. It moves in a completely opposite direction to the usual, including provisions that clearly relate to a reformist orientation of the government project. The word'reform' is not used here euphemistically. These are regulations aimed at changing the tax landscape with the ultimate aim of growth. The long-standing and much-discussed development will not only come with major projects and investments. It will also come with the creation of a stable, secure and friendly tax system that treats taxpayers fairly. A relationship of confidence and cooperation between the state and the workers, pensioners and entrepreneurs is being structured firmly and decisively. We are all partners, associates and not opponents in this dynamic effort to reconstruct the country. Thank you very much.

It has four thousand nine thousand Greek vessels, controls 20.67% of world tonnage and 54.28% of European tonnage. So we are talking about a superpower internationally, but also at European level. How many ships are with Greek flags? Just the five hundred ninety-nine. So only 12% of the Greek fleet is under Greek flag. Specifically, I will tell you that only three hundred ships choose the Cypriot flag, while over seven hundred fifty ships choose the Maltese flag and, of course, various other flags such as Liberia, Panama, the Marshall Islands. At the same time, I would like to tell you that in 1995, when the Greek fleet totaled two thousand nine hundred and thirty-nine ships, ships flying the Greek flag were a thousand nineteen. In other words, the Greek fleet rate on the Greek owner was 42% and today it is 12%. In addition, the Greek flag offers a prestigious presence in the European institutions, but mainly in the International Maritime Organisation. You know, in this Chamber, we need to speak with certain numbers. Of course that's not an accident. We want them to be employed in the seagoing shipping of Greek birds. We must not deprive anyone of the right to be able to engage in Greek shipping. They're the ones who answer if $2,200 is a gallon's salary. Now, what happens to global shipping, to know what we're talking about. Note: for Greek-owned vessels with a foreign flag, international collective labour agreements apply. How are these concluded? They are concluded with International Transport Workers Federation, known as ITF, and International Maritime Employers' Council. So, what are we going to do? As is true of Greek-owned vessels with a foreign flag, we must also apply them to Greek-owned vessels with a Greek flag. I think that in this way - it is quite clear - we will also strengthen the competitiveness of the Greek register, which is constantly decreasing, but we will also attract new unemployed Greeks, who are at the moment looking for work on seagoing boats. This very simple thing we're doing today. In addition, in a second provision, which we introduce, we allow the Greek shipowner, again on board the Greek flag, when there is no work offered by the Greek master, to allow him, in order not to change his flag, to charter a foreign master. Here I come to say that there is also a huge problem finding quality officers in global shipping. According to the latest investigation of the International Chamber of Shipping in cooperation with BIMCO, it has been established that by 2025 there will be a deficit in officers of over 40,000 five hundred, in relation to ever demand. So, glorious glorious field! We have the world's first shipping, we have five thousand pit ships, we have to enable the productive schools to produce more quality officers, because I told you that the needs are very great. In practical terms, there is no unemployment among the officers. That's the reality. On the contrary, we must produce more and more quality officers, because global shipping is changing. There are new environmental regulations, new regulations, cybersecurity, digital governance. So we want to upgrade public maritime education as a whole. This objective, which we are implementing, is what we do in cooperation with the Evgenid Foundation, which we are preparing the main strategic axes so that we can upgrade overall with a plan of integrated maritime education so that we can produce more quality officers. Furthermore, the last provision proposed for maritime unions ensures respect for the institutionalised principles of transparency, but also reciprocity in the elections, in the antiquities of the trade unions. In other words, to say it and simply to the elections, we want those exercising the naval profession to vote in antiquities rather than what has been observed so far by a representative or by correspondence in writing to take part in elections. We provide, ladies and gentlemen, for an alternative tax on pensions from abroad for all those who will declare Greece as their tax home. We therefore motivate foreign pensioners to transfer their tax home to Greece, since they will be taxed at a rate of 7% for a period of ten years and to exempt them from the special solidarity levy, a measure that has already been successfully implemented in several European Union countries, which will make a significant contribution to increasing public revenue. We are introducing a suspension of the obligation to pay a trade fee for farmers under normal conditions. It shall thus be implemented by the Government its commitment to exempt farmers from the trade charge, in particular for the fiscal year 2019 and after five years from the date of accession to the normal VAT system. We lighten hundreds of thousands of farmers of normal status who joined the normal VAT system as of 1 January 2014 and completed the five-year exemption. Then we reduce the advance income tax. Let's not forget that it was on the SYRIZA government, which was uploaded to 100%. Today we are reducing the advance payment of income tax for 2019 in proportion to the reduction in VAT turnover in the first half of 2020, compared to the first half of 2019 and zeroing it in some cases. We are introducing such arrangements in order to stimulate liquidity so that we can provide financial support for businesses and their sustainability. Ladies and gentlemen, it was SYRIZA in February 2019 as a government that abolished first home protection by law, and formed such a framework, which did not guarantee protection for all borrowers. With the Bridge Programme we give a deep breath to borrowers of first home housing loans, with the decisive involvement of the public. The New Democracy Government is setting up a new loan grant programme, including up-to-date borrowers, because we are aware of the situation in which they have come because of the health crisis. The previous government provided protection for only six months and only for specific borrowers. Who covered? In addition, the Commission considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market within the meaning of Article 107(3)(c) of the Treaty. With the new programme, the beneficiaries are significantly enlarged in relation to the former government's first home protection programme. The new arrangement includes natural persons, workers in affected sectors, unemployed who receive exceptional support, free professionals in affected sectors, natural persons who have suffered a reduction in wages. We include professionals who have had a reduction in their commercial activity, beneficiaries who received aid in the form of the repayable advance, natural persons who are part of the mechanism to strengthen the employment of “COMPAN - WORK” and property owners who receive a reduced rent. We provide state subsidies for both service-related and non-performing loans. With this programme we reward the consistent borrowers with serviced loans, which have been affected by the health crisis. We are subsidising an important part of the monthly instalment of the loan, up to EUR 600. We thus limit the risk of creating a new generation of reds and loans because of the pandemic. Then we abolish the tax rate of 10% of the financial parental benefits and donations from parents to their children, with the aim of rekindling the real estate market. Ladies and gentlemen, at this point I would like to highlight the quickly reflexes of the Ministry on major issues of society, and I will refer to a local issue of the Amphithea community of the Ioannina Prefecture, where, with the existing coefficient of land exploitation that emerged, the EMI was disproportionately disproportionate to the real objective value of the properties, a misrepresentation due both to the incorrect registration of the number of residents of the settlement and to the price of the zone. There was even a taxpayer who was asked to pay 30,000 to 35,000 euros a year for a few acres of land. Since last October we have been in constant contact with the relevant ministers and the Secretary-General of the Ministry in order to examine the matter in detail and to correct the injustice that has been committed in recent years to property owners in Amphithea and we are very close to resolving this issue, after the ministerial decision has been pending in the coming days. I would like to thank the Ministers in particular. In the difficult circumstances created by the pandemic of the coronary, we continue consistently, responsibly and methodically our reform work. We are serving society and the citizens and we are determined to exhaust every financial margin to stimulate the market, to support businesses and workers, to strengthen the Greek society and economy, in order to address the adverse effects of the coronary pandemic on the business environment. We stand by those who have been most affected by the unprecedented reality that we are experiencing. We prove that we are listening to society and its problems. All movements by the Government and the Ministry responsible show the truth. Society will remain united and upright. Thank you.

This very dual objective also serves the current bill of the Ministry of Finance, which, with targeted interventions, seeks to relieve citizens and businesses of the consequences of the pandemic and, on the one hand, to create a favourable tax environment that attracts investment and promotes growth. So, in particular, a regulation with a clear development mark is what enables pensioners from foreign countries, particularly in Europe, to transfer their tax residence to Greece, giving them the incentive to lower taxation at a rate of 7% on their earnings. This is a measure with a high development perspective both for our tourism and for the real estate market, as it is expected that a number of these pensioners will turn to either the market or the long-term rental of homes in our country with multiple benefits for both the national and local economies. As far as Europe is concerned, according to EUROSTAT, the population of over sixty years amounted to around 183 million in 2017 and by 2050 it will reach 247 million. In fact, the revelation of today's regulation has already been promoted in the European press, making it a special feeling. I cannot say at this point that this regulation is an excellent opportunity for my country, Pieria, a tourist destination, with a large number of accommodations suitable for retired people's residence, but also with infrastructures for health, entertainment and support which people are usually looking for. Above all, it provides a natural environment with unparalleled and ideal weather conditions that make it one of the most attractive destinations. Further regulation with also a clear development dimension is the abolition of taxation with 10% of the financial parental benefits and donations for the first home purchase of up to EUR 150,000. It is a measure that facilitates young people to acquire their first home, but also strengthens the real estate market and the business sectors that are active around it. The bill includes a number of tax reliefs. In addition, the provision of Article 18, which reduces or even zeros the advance tax payment for 2019 is also crucial. Finally, with the GFCF programme and the relative subsidy for government housing loan instalments, a significant breath is given to thousands of borrowers and, for the first time, the regulatory framework is expanded so that the consequent borrowers are also covered by this arrangement. The tax bill we are debating today establishes measures with a development dimension with a strong social and environmental footprint. I am thinking of reducing the fee for classifying private car passengers and trucks through key and substantial interventions. In particular, a new system of progressive taxation of IX cars is being introduced. The charges are now linked to the old times of the vehicle, the registration fee for low pollutant hybrid cars is reduced and the historical fee and the relevant committee which has caused many delays in the clearance of used cars is abolished. This is a number of provisions which, at the same time, aim to strengthen the car market. In addition, the Government has consistently made progress in reducing the tax burden policy for the tax year 2019 in suspending the payment of the trade fee for the main industrial farmers of the normal scheme. Similarly, for the tax year 2019 the payment of the trade fee and for coastal fishing fishermen operating either individually or in the form of a co-ownership or civil society fishing vessels up to 12 metres between hakes shall be suspended. In the same direction, zeroing or reducing the tax advance for the companies affected by the pandemic moves. In particular, there will be no obligation to pay the tax advance for seasonal enterprises that made more than 50% of their annual turnover in the quarter of July, August, 2019, a measure aimed at alleviating the business of tourism in particular. At the same time, escalating reductions in the company tax advance will apply depending on the percentage of their turnover reduction. The percentage of the reduction ranges from 30% to 70% and even zeroing the tax advance for enterprises whose turnover reduction rate exceeds 35%. Another measure of tax relief, but also with a strong social focus, is included in the regulation that autonomous taxation is abolished at a rate of 10% of the financial parental benefits and donations from parents to their children in order to use them for the purchase of first homes. It is radically changing the way in which taxation is now to take place in accordance with the scales laid down in Article 29 of Law No 2961/2009, which also provides for a tax-free of EUR 150,000. Two objectives are achieved. First, the tax burden is reduced for the large majority of parents who have a family benefit to their children and second, the first-stage purchase is reinforced. Furthermore, there are beneficial provisions to support sport that as a sector has been particularly affected, such as the simulation in the taxation of coaches' incomes with that of athletes and the reduction in the VAT rate for tickets from 24% to 13%. The development measure is also the strong incentive we give to pensioners with a tax home abroad in order to transfer their tax residence to Greece and to subject them to an alternative tax on income of foreign origin, with a tax rate of 7% for a period of 15 years, while exempting them from the special solidarity levy. Developmental dimension is the rule that shares granted free of charge by companies primarily to their employees - and not exclusively to their employees, as some people claim - are taxed at a rate of 15% for the goodwill earned by the employee from selling those shares. This allows businesses to make use of a means of attracting, rewarding and maintaining skilled workers, which has so far been practically inactive because of the unfavourable tax burden on the worker. As a result, workers will also be encouraged to become more productive in favour of the company in which they work and in the long run the economy as a whole. At the same time and in the light of the new developments which call for the strengthening of internal tourism, the income tax exemption for workers of interactive domestic tourism of a total value of up to EUR 300 provided by their employers is introduced. As I said before, this regulation does not simply extend the exemptions for workers from the calculation of wage earners' income but also aims to strengthen internal tourism. I would also like to refer to an important innovation, to the establishment of the Committee on External Economic Relations, on tax disputes. Through the functioning of this committee, thousands of cases will be resolved out of court which are currently pending in the Council of State and in the ordinary administrative courts. The aim is to dismantle administrative justice, quickly resolve outstanding tax disputes and stimulate tax revenues. This committee is an effective and rapid out-of-court mechanism to resolve tax disputes pending before the Council of State and the administrative courts. The committee will be based in Athens, while an annex of the committee will be operating in Thessaloniki. The Committee will operate in three-member chambers consisting of two former judges of the administrative courts with a degree of at least one year and one member of the board of the State. This composition shall ensure full impartiality and high quality of the work of the Committee. Ladies and gentlemen, you will allow me to refer to the amendment tabled by a general number of 422 and a special 57, and the provisions of which deal with issues arising out of the sad juncture but also problems that should have been dealt with for years. A few days ago we had the sad incident of the attack against employees of the Kozani DU in their workplace. Three workers were seriously injured and one of them continues to fight for his life. Other instances of violence against tax administration officials have been recorded in the performance of their duties. I believe that the condemnation of these acts of violence is given by all the wings of the House. The state's moral obligation is to stand alongside the employees of the DU Kozani and their families, and this will be done in practice. The amendment has included a provision that the Independent Authority for Public Revenue will undertake to cover all the costs of hospitalisation and rehabilitation of officials who suffer physical injury or damage to their health as a result of an attack on them during and in the course of their duties. There is also provision for the cadets who are under attack, but by the staff code they are not entitled to a sick leave before six months have elapsed since their appointment. In such cases, it is foreseen that these officials are, exceptionally, duly absent and that the Independent Public Revenue Authority will undertake to cover their monthly earnings until the necessary genuine public service of six months has been completed in order to establish the right to sick leave. The state's essential and institutional obligation is to protect the officials of the tax administration and to send a strict message to all those who exercise violence to prevent controls. This is the purpose of the provisions that tighten the framework of sanctions imposed on phenomena of physical violence against auditors of the Independent Public Income Authority and also employees of the Ministry of Finance. A special fine of EUR 10 000 to EUR 50,000 is envisaged for the perpetrator as well as the suspension of the business establishment from two to six months, and in the event of a relapse six months to three years is to be suspended. In the direction of increasing the index of tax compliance, the provision that the framework for non-conservation or preservation of accounting records is strictly applied shall be initiated. It should be noted that these provisions seek to impose proportionate penalties on those taxpayers who refuse to cooperate with the tax authority and provide absolutely no evidence during the investigation. On the other hand, since the determination of the taxable material is possible by all appropriate means, the special fine shall not be imposed. The protection of legitimate and cooperative taxpayers is thus achieved. A stricter fine shall also be introduced for cases where tax electronic mechanisms are not safeguarded, as well as records and memories thereof, or for cases where they are attempted to violate, modify or interfere with their operation. The tax returns shall be extended until 28 August 2020 by the appropriate adjustment to the repayment instalments. Doses remain eight. At the same time, the 2% deduction for the one-off payment of the income tax on natural persons, to be effected by 31 August 2020, is maintained, while a correct calculation of the reduced advance payment of income tax on tax returns is ensured for 2019. The state comes to subsidise the loans of people affected by the pandemic, whether they are workers in businesses that have been affected either by free trade or by entrepreneurs themselves. Subsidies for nine months of the loan, between EUR 300 and EUR 600 per month in an environment that we must not forget. Because there is a lot of talk of protecting the first home, we must not forget that, as the Minister said earlier, there was no protection of the first home in Greece today. The scheme expired in February 2019. Those who find that it is not enough to protect the first home were those who have nowadays ended the protection, and as we have heard the Prime Minister say today, in the Eurogroup, the previous government was committed that there would be no extension of the law of protection that existed. Nowadays, there has been some extension, and in any case, with the effort to tackle the pandemic, there is this programme as well. The second very great relief coming from this bill, which concerns a lot of people and one of the major obstacles to the Greek economy, is the reduction of the tax advance. The advance tax payment, I have said it many times, is a real hardship for the private sector, for the business of the country that produces wealth, because for years they have been called upon to pay taxes for profits that they do not know if they will have. Now, then, there is a regulation whereby those in the first half of 2020, based on their turnover figures, based on VAT returns, have a reduction of over 35%, will not pay any tax advance at all. And there is an escalation of reductions for those with smaller turnover reductions. This is very important. It is, of course, part of a wider policy that we must see and then, Minister. We have announced this not as a measure in relation to the pandemic, it is part of our programme, to reduce the advance payment of tax in general and not just for the affected companies and free traders. I would like to make a specific reference, Mr Minister, to this point. There is a special case, I told you in committee, where companies that in the third quarter of 2019 present 50% of their turnover, so those that are purely tourists are exempted from the tax advance. That's right. But you have determined that this is only about the tertiary sector. There are similar cases in the primary and secondary ones and we must this Greece that produces primarily to strengthen. A pensioner may bring his tax residence to Greece and pay 7% tax for only fifteen years. I've also seen the legislative improvement he's come to. Here it is to return money to the country. The provision as it is, I'm afraid, does not ensure that. I think we should deal with a further piece of legislation. There is wealth of Greeks abroad that can come to Greece - and I am not talking about the wealth that has illegally left in the years of the crisis or I do not know what - there is legally acquired wealth abroad and we need to find ways to come to Greece. The current draft law, ladies and gentlemen, achieves these objectives. I would like to focus on two main aspects of this draft law which, in my opinion, demonstrate the importance of today's legislative initiative. The first relates to the mesh of tax interventions promoted through this bill and will have beneficial effects for Greek taxpayers. For example, through this draft law we provide incentives for pensioners who have a tax home abroad to transfer their residence to Greece. We abolish the self-taxation of 10% of the financial parental benefits from parents to their children so that the property market is rekindled. We are reducing or even zeroing the advance tax payment for 2019 depending on the fall of the turnover shown this year by companies in order to strengthen the liquidity of businesses. We are exempting farmers in particular for the tax year 2019 from the obligation to pay the trade fee. Finally, it is particularly important to set up a Committee on Foreign Affairs to resolve outstanding tax disputes before the Council of State and the ordinary administrative courts. Our aim is to facilitate the rapid resolution of these disputes, to achieve the dismantling of administrative justice and, of course, to stimulate tax revenue. And I come to the second important part of this draft law concerning the "GEYRA" programme and the protection of first residence. It should also be recalled that it was the Government of the New Democracy which extended by seven months the protection framework until 31-07-20, and today, in view of the situation of borrowers as a result of the health crisis, we are creating a new loan grant programme involving an important innovation. We broaden the beneficiaries and include, for the first time, informed borrowers. We are therefore subsidising both non-performing loans and, indeed, non-performing loans after 31/12-2018 which were not covered by the previous government, as well as subsidized loans. For the first time, ladies and gentlemen, a government rewards the citizens who are consistent with credit obligations which have been affected by the pandemic in this country. And I think that this regulation also says a lot about the philosophy that the New Democracy Government is giving up, but also about the policy it is serving. At the same time we encourage borrowers to directly regulate their debts with banks and loan managers, while preventing the abuse of the new framework by strategic poor payers. Because our aim is not to create a new generation of red loans in the midst of a pandemic crisis, but to protect households and social groups that are vulnerable and need support from the state. Finally, I wanted to explain at this point why specific borrowers such as firefighters cannot join the GEOFER programme. These citizens - as you know - have received a loan in previous years with a state guarantee and therefore, because of European state aid legislation, they cannot receive state aid again.
